## CHAPTER SUMMARIES — VOLUME 03, BATCH 0004 (Chapters 131–140), "The Woman Who Was Carried Out, And The Sealed District"

**The one batch in this volume that crosses a movement boundary. Chapters 131, 132 and 133 are the last three of Movement 3 and close it on Sunday 19 September 2025. Chapters 134 to 140 are the first seven of Movement 4 and open it on Monday 20 September. The change of register between them is the change of register in the case and is not announced on the page.**

## Chapter 0131 — The Post Box, And Not A Form

**Fri 10 – Sat 11 Sep 2025, and the first of October is twenty days away on the Saturday.**

**The September recovery page for the district of Ferry Bank goes out: one side of A4, about nine hundred addresses, three paragraphs, no name in the third of them.** Paragraph three says that the office has not established whom it may speak for on behalf of the residents of the district and is asking, and that the place for replies is a post box in Wharf Street, and that you do not have to give your name and do not have to write anything on the sheet at all. **The two words *and is asking* were fought for over about eleven minutes on 31 July by a resilience coordinator of forty-seven, in the fourth item of a signed minute, and a claims handler of thirty-four has said out loud in a room that the paragraph is not about the residents and is about a person at a desk who has to type a sentence and find a meeting.**

**A woman of thirty-four says *the City Flood Office has consulted with the residents* out loud and refuses to have it typed, twice, and a typist of forty-seven who has typed every publication out of that office since 2019 refuses as well and gives the better reason: the sentence would be true when typed and false by the time anybody looked at it, and the only person who did that would be a clerk of forty-seven working late. She then says the sentence this chapter turns on — *the two best arguments we have got for anything in this flood have both come from the people whose whole job is the typing and the saying of a form* — and nobody improves on it, and he crosses two thirds of it out of his own notebook and leaves the last sentence.**

**THE TURN, AND IT TOOK A PAVEMENT.** A pillar box is not a postal address. There is no such thing as posting to one. What the sheet has given nine hundred people is: put it in the box, and I will find it, because I empty it — and that works, because the reference on the front is what gets it looked at, **and it works for about nine hours, because the box is emptied at six, and after six it is on a van, and then it is a letter, and a letter is sorted, and the only person in the city who can stand at that box and say which envelope is which is a member of the public on a Friday rota, and she is not there on a Saturday.** Four things are in the box by half past one on the Saturday. A claims handler of thirty-four stands on the pavement for forty minutes and does not put her hand in it and says why: then she is a person who read four things a household had not sent anywhere, and she has not asked them whether she may. **Nobody can say who is allowed to open it, and the answer to a question the office cannot ask is a sentence in a letter saying the Post Office has not lost anything, and the filing of that letter is the end of it.**

## Chapter 0132 — The District's First Instrument

**Mon 13 – Tue 14 Sep 2025, and the first of October is seventeen days away on the Tuesday.**

**The first record-status ever applied to a frontage anybody in this case can name.** A housing officer of fifty-eight puts a chainage and a length on a table: `FERRY BANK / WHARF FRONTAGE / CH. 0+31 TO 0+35`, forty feet, and the frontages behind it, and about seven households behind that, and she says the sentence she has been carrying since Friday: **there is no such thing as knowing where a thing is.** A chainage is not a place. A frontage is not a house. A line on a map has a length, a direction and a bearing and has got no people on it. She writes four words on a pad and turns it round: *a line is not a door.*

**On the Tuesday at 10:26 a.m. a duty officer of forty-seven with nineteen years in post completes the block at Northglass, in front of four people who asked to watch.** Premises reference, status at 4 August 2025 — *no occupier recorded* — period affected from 06:00 Sunday 15 August 2025 to 23:59 Thursday 30 September 2025, and her name. She says the reason out loud first, twice: **she has never once been asked whether it is a good idea, it is not her decision, the block is completed by a duty officer and that is printed on it, and she entered it accurately because she would rather it were a bad thing she could be asked about.** She says the front of the form chooses the frontages, that the person who signs the front does not know a status is attached to each address behind the premises, and that the block says so on its own face. **This is the tenth status in this county since 24 June and the first on ground anybody in the room can say out loud.**

**Four people watch and three of them could have stopped it and none of the three has anything.** The duty officer: a status is not a determination, it is not appealable, it goes on a frontage, it is for computation. The housing officer: no instrument, no power, not a party, and she could have put her hand flat on the table about four times in nine minutes. The man with no post: he can say that he put the chainage on a piece of paper on 4 August and did not know what it was for.

**And she is proved right about the ground in the afternoon.** A structural engineer of forty-one levels the same forty feet: about seventy millimetres of fall from north end to south end, and **that is a fall and not a movement and it has probably been there since the wall was built**, and she has known since about 11 August and has given the number to three people in a room and to no document, for the same reason a man of fifty-five has not given a table, and she says she has never met him. **The two professionals agree about the difference between a fall and a movement and neither of them has been told about the other.**

**And it ends with the second half of Chapter 127's finding, from the other side.** There is a household nobody is owed anything for and it cannot be found. **And there are about seven households behind forty feet of wall we can point at on a map and we cannot ask one of them anything, and the method has not changed and the difference is that a housing officer has had a piece of paper in her handbag for four days that says where the frontage is, and it has not helped her once.** That is the finding, and it is a finding and not a failure because it has now happened twice, from both ends. ⚠ **And a woman of fifty-eight says the period ends. He does not, and the chapter says so, and the counting has never been his.**

## Chapter 0133 — Who May Speak For A Community, And It Is Not Settled

**Sat 18 – Sun 19 Sep 2025, and it CLOSES MOVEMENT 3. The first of October is twelve days away on the Sunday.**

**A first-floor room on Wharf Street, eleven people, four of them here because they heard about it and four of those four because they are frightened**, said in that order by a resilience coordinator of forty-seven who has tried for a week to get it to eight. A district nurse of forty-four who could not get away. A man of sixty-one who chairs a tenants' association on two streets and came to ask for a roof and was not honest with. A woman of thirty-six with a childminding place and two parents she has not rung in nine weeks. A parish clerk of fifty who came up from the river. And a woman of sixty-one who has come down a hill three times in about eight weeks and told nobody she was coming.

**She asks the question she said on 7 September she was not going to ask, and tells the room before she asks it that she is not going to answer it.** *When this room has finished today, in about four years, and somebody from an office that has been reorganised twice wants to know what this room said, who is it that they ask?* Four people try. The four answers are about a document, an office, a signature and a rota. **Nobody answered it. She gives no answer at all, and the reason is that she has been asked this question three times in her life and every answer she has ever given has been about a person, and a thing that has to be carried by a person is a rota and not a practice.** She said that before anybody decided it was not one.

**Nine items of work are done in the remaining fifty-five minutes and every one of them is right**: the recovery page went out and nine hundred and forty-one envelopes went, and that is a number about envelopes; a post box with four things in it that the case cannot open; a status at a chainage that cannot be appealed; a page; a roof, from the man of sixty-one, who read the 31 July minute, voted for it, and was not honest with; a ground floor; a dressing, which the district nurse asked not to be recorded; a third paragraph three people have now asked about and none of whom has asked her to change it; and a claim that reached its fortieth day and closed on Monday for want of a date.

**And on the Sunday night, because September arrived, a registrar of twenty-eight telephones a parish registrar at about eleven o'clock** — the asking Hesta Rell permitted in September and not before, for the reason that she came on a Monday morning, asked one question, read a book for two hours, and did not once ask what the registrar believes, and that nobody from that building has done it in five years. **A facsimile of one page comes back, the page for the eleventh of April 1949, four interments, and the four conditions, and the fourth condition is read out twice because Hesta Rell asks for it again.** A registrar of sixty-eight says she would be disappointed if the registrar called it progress, and that the thing that would make it progress is a list, and that the day she is asked for a list she can have one and will not give it to her. **The registrar of twenty-eight says out loud that she is not going to call it progress.** The minute is four items and is the third document in this case that records a question and records that nobody answered it, and its fourth item is the August minute's fourth item, and both of those things were read out on a telephone on a Sunday night to the woman who asked, because in June she said she would be asked in September and not before. **Nothing was opened. No name came back. Nobody was given a house.**

## Chapter 134 — A Mark On A Wall

**Mon 20 – Tue 21 Sep 2025, and MOVEMENT 4 OPENS. The first of October is ten days away. The register of the voices changes here.**

**A man of about fifty-five with a folding rule, a bucket, a spanner and a lump of cement in a plastic basin gets off a bus on the ring road at twenty past eight, uninvited and unasked for, and says so before he puts a spanner anywhere: if it turns out afterwards that this was the case asking me, he will put the spanner in the back of the van and drive off and nobody will see him again until 2031.** He is here because the second instrument ran out at 23:59 on 31 August and nobody renewed it, and there is now four hundred metres of a hundred-and-forty-year-old revetment with nobody's name on it, and he is the only person in the county who walks walls.

**Four brass survey marks go in at chainage 0+00, 0+130, 0+260 and 0+390, with levels above a nail he drove himself at the north end in 2016: 4.031, 4.058, 4.104, 4.149 — a rise of one hundred and eighteen millimetres over three hundred and ninety metres going up the bank.** And nobody can read those numbers except him, and that is on purpose and he says so before he sets the first one: a mark a person can read is a mark in a system, a mark in a system is a mark that can be queried, and a mark that can be queried is a mark that in about four years gets deleted. **A man of thirty-two is offered the book face down on the wall and does not look at it, in about four seconds, and the man with the rule tells him the reason he gave in the summer was not the reason, and it is not standing: he has already had one warm thing come out of an object this year and believed it and a woman of sixty-nine has a line through her name on a public schedule because of it, and he is not holding a book in this county in September.** A man of twenty-five asks the one question he is entitled to ask — who is the second person on a mark — and gets the answer that the book is a job and not a document.

**A caretaker of sixty-three shows a photograph of twenty-three inches of white emulsion off a skirting, painted Friday the ninth of July, and the man of fifty-five says in nine seconds that it is a correct measurement of a floor and not a mark**, and the difference between them is that a mark can be found again in about nine years by a person with a van and a photograph cannot be found again by anybody, ever, because the floor is not the same floor it was. He sets the caretaker a mark in the corridor outside the gym for nothing, at ten past four on the Tuesday, because he asked the right question in a doorway in August.

**And a registrar of twenty-eight says the whole of what this case has in about forty seconds and refuses to speculate about the rest of it: a mark says where the water stood, a family says who was in the room, a claims form wants a day, and there is no arithmetic that turns one into another.** She gives two reasons for not guessing and the second one is about the man in the room. **A man of fifty-five, asked for the sixth time since March whether the pattern is the rope or the pattern is the place, says no — in a room with seven people in it instead of a car park — and says the knots on a rope are a series of marks and a series of marks is a series of heights, and that that is arithmetic and does not tell anybody why anybody tied them, and that the answer will be a number beside a knot in a corridor and it will not be a sentence and nobody will have said anything.**

## Chapter 0135 — Who Was In The Room

**Thu 23 – Fri 24 Sep 2025, and the first of October is seven days away on the Friday.**

**The method is a door, because a form has a box for a household and this question is about a room, and a registrar of twenty-eight has known since 2019 that there is no form in this city in which the thing a question relates to is a room.** She says the other half out loud to the room before anybody goes out: a door can be shut, a form can be filled in wrong, and we are about to be worse than a form. A housing officer of fifty-eight takes eleven addresses off a pad and will not read them out. **The asking is done by a claims handler of thirty-four, because she is the only person in this city who is professionally obliged to be told a fact about a household and has no power over anybody at all.** A man of thirty-two asks to come to one door and is allowed exactly one, and is given the rule in nine words — stand behind whoever is asking, and if anybody asks you a question say you are not the office, and if anybody asks you a second time say it again, because a man who says it once is a man who is about to explain himself.

**The first door is opened at about a quarter to eleven by a woman of about fifty-nine who has been waiting since the nineteenth of June and who is not frightened of anything, and she says that first, and then she asks a claims handler of thirty-four what her job is, and then whether she likes it, and the answer is no, and neither question is about the flood.** Then she is asked her one question — who was in this room on the night of the eighteenth of June — and gives five people and a dog and a man from the gas board who stood in her doorway for about an hour and did not come in and did not go out, and a time to the day, and a date she has had since the nineteenth of June and told nobody.

**The second door: a man of about seventy does not answer for about four minutes, and then says he is not going to be asked anything, and then opens it, and says he was upstairs and asleep on the night of the eighteenth and came down at about six, and his wife was on the step in a coat, and he has not been back in that front room since the twentieth of June.** He is not frightened of the case. **He is frightened of a piece of paper, because on it there will be a line saying a man of seventy was not in his own house while his wife was in a ground-floor front room with the water at the door, and in about four years somebody will ask him about it in a room with nothing in front of him, and his wife is going to be alive. He is not frightened of you and I want that said.** He asks to be left alone and a housing officer who has knocked nine times in twenty-two years and had not knocked here for nine weeks goes.

**Eleven households, about nine hours, one question each. Nine of the eleven gave a time, two gave a time and a day, and NOT ONE OF THE ELEVEN GAVE A HEIGHT**, and nine of them said the same sentence in nine different ways, and one woman of forty-four has a photograph of a silt line on a doorframe, which is a height, and it is a height off a floor in a flat that is not this district's datum, and a man of fifty-five put four brass marks in a wall on Monday at heights above a nail in 2016, and **the two numbers are about four hundred metres and two zeros apart.** A claims handler of thirty-four writes eleven addresses with dates against them in a wallet, says so out loud, has not looked one of them up, and asks a housing officer to say to her face in about four years in case she turns into the person who puts them in a system. **And the two people in this chapter are not the same thing and the chapter says so: one is afraid of a document and one has been waiting since June for a knock, and neither of them is a device.**

## Chapter 0136 — Two Days

**Tue 28 – Wed 29 Sep 2025, and the first of October is two days away at the end of the Wednesday. The register's keeper is eighty-one.** ⚠ **The Batch 0004 prompt gave this chapter as *Mon 28 – Tue 29 Sep* and that is one day low: on the pinned line 28 September is a Tuesday and 29 September is a Wednesday, and Chapter 137's own correct opening on *Thursday the thirtieth* is what proves it. Corrected by the second review pass of 26 September 2026 — weekday labels only, and no date and no interval figure moved, because the dates were right and only the labels were wrong.**

**A postwoman of fifty-two comes to a room above a shop on Monday morning and says, before she sits down, *I have not come to give you anything.* She empties a pillar box at six every evening, has done it for eleven years, has read the front of all six envelopes with the office's reference on them, has put a rubber band round the six and not round the other thirteen, and says a rubber band is not a plan.** A pillar box is not a postal address; there is no such thing as posting to one; the bundle is in a cage on a van and on Wednesday it goes to the cage and a reference on the front of a piece of paper is a thing a machine reads. **She is not a record and she does not want to be one and she says she would have said no to a form in June, and gives the reason, and the reason is the reason this case has been using for five months.**

**The case drafts a covering letter with a schedule enclosed, and the schedule is not designed by anybody in this case — it comes out of a system off a template in use since about 2016.** A housing officer of fifty-eight says *that is a form* in about three seconds and she is right. **On the Tuesday morning a registrar of twenty-eight says the whole thing in four sentences: we do not send it, because a schedule filled in by a postwoman makes her the only person in this city holding a record of what other people put in a public box, and she declined that, and if we do not send it then she has no record either, and that is the same thing that happened to a woman of sixty-nine with a card and a woman of about forty-three who was put on a schedule in a hall and has never been asked. There is no third door, and I have been looking for one since the first of June, and she asks for three names on the decision and not for the office.**

**And on the Monday afternoon the reason nobody in this case has said in a room what is going to happen on Friday is said out loud, in about four minutes, and the reason is not restraint.** Two reasons and they are the same reason: this case has four instruments and every one of them is a document, and the only instrument in this flood that is not a document is a sentence in a room; and **nobody in this case knows what the date is for, and four of us inventing what a date is for in a room is the one thing this case must not do.** A registrar of twenty-eight will not minute it, because a minute is read out to a person who was not in the room and there are two people on this island who would be read it and neither of them is in this case.

**The book is referred to and neither of its dates is printed.** A hardback of about a hundred and fifty ruled pages, a pound and ten, no names in it, about nine gaps, about four hundred more things to come, a drawer that does not lock on purpose, and a fourth line on the first page addressed to nobody in particular: *if you are reading this and you are not me, then you are the second person, and that is the only reason it exists.* **There are two days in it that do not agree with each other and a repair pass has not chosen between them, and a writer who printed either of them would be making a fact.** The line is the only sentence in five months of this case that is a rule about a document and not a rule about a person, and a man of twenty-five wrote the same sentence in four pages in a building on a hill in December and nobody reads it either. **He is not going up the hill, and he says the eighth time he notices is not going to be a knock.**

## Chapter 0137 — Friday The First Of October

**Thu 30 Sep – Fri 1 Oct 2025, three days, and the second day IS the date and it is not an event.**

**A boundary stops and a register ends and nobody appointed anybody.** The writing is five lines of white emulsion on the brick flank of the old mill yard at the back of Sillick Lane, about nine feet high, at the height of five feet, put on with a roller and a bolt pen in about eleven minutes on 24 April 2025 by a man of fifty-two who lives at number 4, unsigned, with a line for a name on it that is empty and is staying empty. Line five: *nobody signs it. Nobody keeps it. It is on the wall.* **Pinned a foot to the right of it is a sheet of A4 with the same five lines in a different hand, made in about eleven minutes one evening in May by a man of about thirty-four who lives at number 8 because he thought people wanted to know what the wall said, and its fifth line says *somebody signs it, somebody keeps it*, and it is wrong, and it is staying wrong.** A registrar of twenty-eight said out loud in a hall on 28 June 2025 that she was not going to take it down, and gave the reason: if it comes off, in about four years there is one account of the fifth line and it is on a wall in a yard where you cannot get to it in the wet, and a woman of eighty-one is going to be asked whether the fifth line says what she thinks it says.

**It rained from two o'clock. Nine people are in the yard and two of them came for a cup of tea and one of them came for a bus, and the whole of the organisation was a man at number 4 putting his head out of his back door and saying *are you coming down* to nobody in particular.** They stood in the rain and looked at the wall. Nobody read anything out. A woman of about sixty-four said *well* at about half past two and what she meant has never been established because nobody has asked. **The practice at Sillick Lane had three rules and no form under it, it ran from three o'clock until you could not read a number off a door, and it was not running on the first of October, and nobody had decided that.**

**In the kitchen at number 6, for five and a half hours, the only words anybody said were a man of thirty-two's: *It is Friday*, at about ten in the morning, because she had the date written and had her hand near the pen and had stopped; and *right*, at about four in the afternoon, when she turned the book round. Neither of them was a question. He has said no to eight things in about nine weeks and this was the ninth, and the chapter says the ninth was the first one that was not a refusal.**

**THE LAST ENTRY IS `141. 1 October 2025. — this is the last one.`** It is on about the hundred and ninth page of a hardback of about a hundred and fifty ruled pages, so there are about forty-one pages left in it, and about four hundred more things to come, and the arithmetic has not worked since about the eleventh of July. **The book did not run out. The book stopped, and that is the only difference there has ever been between a date of loss and a date.** Nobody put a hand on the book. Nobody asked her what the four hundred were for. Nobody has asked her since.

**And on Thursday night at about eleven o'clock, in his own hand, in his own notebook: he noticed it for the eighth time, asked for nothing for the eighth time, and wrote down beside it that this was the last entry the notebook would make about that day before the day arrived.** The real first of October in the real world is a Wednesday, and the first of October in this case is a Friday, and the only person who says a weekday out loud in the next eleven hours is him, and he says it once.

## Chapter 0138 — A Second Book

**Mon 4 – Tue 5 Oct 2025. The batch's turn, and it is a consequence and not a resolution.**

**A woman of fifty-seven with a bad ankle brings an exercise book to a counter in a claims office on Wharf Street and offers it to the City Flood Office. It is the best document anybody has produced in this flood and the office cannot have it and cannot refuse it and both halves took about eleven seconds.** Four columns and no heading and no name at the top of it. A claims assistant of twenty-nine asks the only question that mattered — *who is the data controller* — and nobody in the building can answer it.

**THE REASON IT EXISTS, IN HER OWN WORDS.** In June a man with a clipboard stood in her front room and asked her for the day her door stopped working, and she could not tell him, and he wrote something on the clipboard, and he did not say out loud that he had not got it, and he went. **She has had four months of that.** About four hundred people have been asked for a date on this low bank and about four hundred can produce a number, and they are producing it at about one a day, on their own, and nobody has asked them and nobody will. **She has no names in it, and she worked that out in about a week, and the reason she worked it out is a dog** — a woman on this street put her sister, her sister's two children and a dog into a contents box on a printed form in a public hall in August because there was nowhere else to put them, and the man who printed that sheet was in the building that morning and did not know.

**Two refusals, four seconds apart, neither of them a rule.** Nobody in this case may touch that book — not the office, not the case, not a register, not a schedule, and not him — and nobody in this case may say no to it, because saying no is a thing we would have to do to a person, and a registrar of twenty-eight has been waiting since about the eleventh of July for a person to come and ask her for a thing, and she is not going to be the person who says no, and if she says no then she stops, and if she stops then there is nothing. **By Tuesday evening it has forty-one households in it.**

**And the finding, in about four sentences, which a registrar got wrong for four months and corrected on a Monday afternoon.** A form wants a number out of a person standing in a wet room on a bad day, and the case spent four months proving that the form is right and the person is right and the two cannot be put in one sentence — **and then a woman with a bad ankle and an exercise book for sixty pence took the whole of the argument and put it somewhere it works, and it works because it has a heading and does not have a heading, and because it has forty-one households and no names, and because the only thing it asks of anybody is that they write down a number.**

**And the sentence that has to be said about nine hundred times over the next three months is *you are right, and we cannot use it*, and it has been said eleven times since 5 July and all forty of the times it has been said it was in a room, and a registrar of twenty-eight says out loud that in three months it will be said in a street, about nine hundred times, and a sentence in a street cannot be checked, and there is no form for a second person and she is not asking for one. And she wanted to send a leaflet about this and nobody in the room would sign it, including her.** A man of thirty-two is asked whether he wants to see the book and says no in about four seconds, and gives two reasons, and only one of them is the real one.

## Chapter 0139 — A Lawyer's Cover Note

**Thu 7 – Fri 8 Oct 2025. The argument is correct and nobody in the chapter says it is wrong and nobody says it is right either.**

**A partner of fifty reads a covering note and a four-clause schedule out loud in a first-floor room on Civic Hill at about two o'clock on the Thursday, and the instrument does not exist and has not been drafted, and the note is privileged and no copy has been made and none is to be made.** Clause one: the instrument will not say that any person is not present. Clause two: it will **seal** the legal description of the district and not the district, and a tenancy, a rate, a policy, a claim, a register entry and a door do not depend on a description and will continue in force and every letter any of them produces will be correct. Clause three: a compensation roll is a set that has to close and this district cannot close, and a roll computed on that basis is not a smaller roll, it is a different one, and it will pay about two hundred and thirty households seven hundred and fifty pounds for a flood that did not reach them and will pay nothing to about nine hundred who were in it. **Clause four: a person in this district will not be hard to find after this. There is an address, a door, a tenancy. What there is not, afterwards, is any instrument in this city in which the question *who was in this building on the night* may be put by anybody and compelled of anybody, and in about four years that will be a thing people believe existed and could not be located, and the reason it cannot be located will be that it was never permitted to be asked.**

**The difference, said out loud by a housing officer of fifty-eight who has been saying it in rooms since about the first of September and who did not have the clause on 7 August when she said it first: a person who is hard to find is a person whose existence somebody may deny; a person with no legal existence is a person whose existence nobody needs to deny.** A partner of fifty says that clause four was drafted on Tuesday and that a structural engineer of forty-one said the same sentence on a wharf on Monday, and that she would like it minuted that the two were not the same, because in about four years two professionals are going to say they were obviously the same.

**And the man of thirty-two with no post is the seventh person in the room and the only one who is not a party to anything, and the partner asks him what the fourth clause does to a person, and does not give him the answer, and writes it in her own book.** It does not hurt anybody who is at an address. **It takes away the only question. A form has a box that wants a day, a schedule has a box that wants a period, a licence has a box that wants a person, and this flood has spent five months looking for a box with a room in it and there is not one, and clause four does not make the line in about nine hundred claim files false. It makes it unnecessary. And a thing that is unnecessary is much harder to notice than a thing that is wrong.**

**A Director of fifty-three is in the room and is not an ally and was sent for by a solicitor and not by anybody in this case.** She is asked whether the Museum holds records about people who have not been told, and says three, in a folder on a second shelf of a cabinet in a registrar's office, in a private series and not a Museum one, and that she has known for five years that they should be, and has neither destroyed nor produced them. She lost an argument about that in about 1998 in a room on the floor below because she was right, and being right is not a thing anybody can do anything with. **Her last thing is the worst thing in the chapter: it will not be a belief, it will be a record — a file in which a question was asked and the answer recorded as not obtainable, true, produced lawfully, and in about forty years somebody will open it and will not be able to tell that the question was never permitted to be asked. She says she would rather say *I do not know* in a room of seven than have anybody in this flood believe she has been on our side since June, because she has not.** Nobody spoke to her for the rest of the afternoon and that was correct.

**And a man of forty-four with a spreadsheet of four hundred and six buildings says that if the description of the district is suspended then a thing that has no description cannot be in a boundary, and a building not in a boundary is not in the district, and in about four years somebody will run a query and get a district with a hole in it the shape of four hundred metres of wall — and they will not be able to see the hole, because the hole is where the frontages are, and the frontages are where the people are.** He asks for a minute with somebody with a duty. There is no minute. A registrar wrote it in her own book and a woman of forty-four has not been told. **The word *seal* is written once, on the schedule, and was said once in that room, in the course of reading the second clause, and it was not used again.**

## Chapter 0140 — The Week Before

**Mon 11 – Tue 12 Oct 2025, and it is thirteen days before the memory cost, and it does not spend one hour of it.**

**Six people, eleven items, two days, and not one single thing went wrong.** A printed agenda produced by a resilience coordinator of forty-seven who is not in the room and has not been in any room since 28 September and has sent a note saying *I am not convening anything today and if anybody convenes anything I will resign, and I have said that in writing and the minute of the thirty-first of July stands.* Three items are decisions and two of the three decisions are about not doing something: the reply arrangements are unchanged; the schedule prepared on 28 September is to be **destroyed unrecorded**, signed by three names and not by an office, and a housing officer of fifty-eight says out loud that it is the one kind of document this flood has produced that nobody can appeal and nobody can be harmed by; **and the eleven doors of 23 and 24 September are not to be visited a second time before the end of the year, because a second visit from an office makes the first one a record, and a housing officer of fifty-eight who wanted to look has not looked and wants it minuted that she wanted to.** There has not been a minute since 5 July.

**A man of forty-four with a file of four hundred and six buildings asks for four numbers and is refused — by a structural engineer of forty-one on the third occasion, because he said *refuse him*, and by the room on the fourth, because nobody said anything.** A man of twenty-five counts the five people in this flood who have wanted a number out of an instrument and have not got one, and proposes nothing, and puts the five in a folder to be found in about 2031, and says that there is no minute and that this is the arrangement and it has been the arrangement for five months.

**He was good at the work and everybody said he was fine and everybody was right.** He reads a claim queue in order and takes a correction on a date and reads on from the right place. And at about half past eleven on the Monday he says the thing about order — a household is a person at an address on a date, a date out of order is the same date in a different place, and there is no form for whether a list of dates is in the right order, and a form for that would be a form for whether a person is a person — and nobody remarks on it, and a claims handler of thirty-four says it is the whole of the case in nine seconds. **Nothing gone. No minute out. No name. No time. No second entry of consequence. Nobody has offered him a word of his own to stop on and nobody is going to.**

**And on the Tuesday afternoon at about four o'clock he goes behind the machines at a laundrette on the low bank.** It is the only warm dry building within about two hundred metres of nine households whose ground floors have not dried since June. There is a rota in pencil on the back of the counter, in the hand of the woman who owns the building, naming days and hours and a person against each slot, with no addresses on it, and it is not this case's document and nothing in this case has written a word of it, and a housing officer of fifty-eight looks at it for about nine seconds and says *that is a rota and not a list* and says nothing else. **And he asks the woman who owns the building to stand in that room in the week of Monday the twenty-fifth of October 2025 — Monday, Tuesday and Wednesday, the twenty-fifth to the twenty-seventh — and read back anything he says, and does not tell her why, and she says yes before he has finished the sentence, and gives three words and a bad reason, and he gives the real one, which is that a man with no post has nobody.**
